e_{k+1}=1\mid\mathsf{n}\odot\mathit{y_{\mathit{i}}}:\mathit{T_{\mathit{i}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\mathit{y_{\mathit{i}}}\colon\mathit{T_{\mathit{i}}}

yields the judgments

r_{1}\sigma_{1},\mathellipsis,r_{k}\sigma_{k},q\mid\mathsf{N}_{1},\mathellipsis,\mathsf{N}_{k},\mathsf{n}\and\Delta_{1},\mathellipsis,\Delta_{k},y_{1}:T_{1}\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}[\mathit{e_{\mathit{i}}}/\mathit{x_{\mathit{i}}}]\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:T\and r_{1}\sigma_{1},\mathellipsis,r_{k}\sigma_{k},q\mid\mathsf{N}_{1},\mathellipsis,\mathsf{N}_{k},\mathsf{n}\and\Delta_{1},\mathellipsis,\Delta_{k},y_{2}:T_{2}\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}[\mathit{e_{\mathit{i}}}/\mathit{x_{\mathit{i}}}]\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:T.

Now applying the rule
Sum.e
yields the desired result.

{case}

[
Raise.i
]
The derivation has the shape

\frac{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}\and\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{M}\and\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{T}}{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}}\mathit{t}\colon\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}}\mathit{T}}

The inductive hypothesis is

\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\sigma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathellipsis,\mathit{r_{\mathit{k}}}\sigma_{\mathit{k}}\mid\mathsf{N}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathellipsis,\mathsf{N}_{\mathit{k}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathellipsis,\Delta_{\mathit{k}}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}[\mathit{e_{\mathit{i}}}/\mathit{x_{\mathit{i}}}]\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{T}.

If we can apply the rule
Raise.i
,
we will obtain the desired result.
But first we must verify that
\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{N}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathellipsis,\mathsf{N}_{\mathit{k}}
.
To do so, it is sufficient to verify
\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{N}_{\mathit{i}}
for each
i
.
But this follows from transitiviy of
\leq
and the fact that

\mathsf{n}<\mathsf{m}_{i}<\mathsf{N}_{i}
.
Where the second inequality is a consequence of the assumption

\sigma_{\mathit{i}}\mid\mathsf{N}_{\mathit{i}}\odot\Delta_{\mathit{i}}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}_{\mathit{i}}}\mathit{e_{\mathit{i}}}\colon\mathit{A_{\mathit{i}}}
.

{case}

[
Raise.e
]
The derivation has the form

\frac{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\mathit{t}\colon\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}}\mathit{T}}{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\operatorname{\uparrow}^{-1}_{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}}\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{T}}

The inductive hypothesis is

\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\sigma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathellipsis,\mathit{r_{\mathit{k}}}\sigma_{\mathit{k}}\mid\mathsf{N}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathellipsis,\mathsf{N}_{\mathit{k}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathellipsis,\Delta_{\mathit{k}}\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}[\mathit{e_{\mathit{i}}}/\mathit{x_{\mathit{i}}}]\mathit{t}\colon\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}}\mathit{T}

and applying the rule
Raise.e
yields the desired result.

{case}

[
Drop.i
]
The derivation has the shape

\frac{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}\and\mathit{q}\in R_{\mathsf{m}}\and\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{T}}{\mathit{q}\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{T}}

The inductive hypothesis is

\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\sigma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathellipsis,\mathit{r_{\mathit{k}}}\sigma_{\mathit{k}}\mid\mathsf{N}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathellipsis,\mathsf{N}_{\mathit{k}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathellipsis,\Delta_{\mathit{k}}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}[\mathit{e_{\mathit{i}}}/\mathit{x_{\mathit{i}}}]\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{T}

and applying the rule
Drop.i
yields the desired result.

{case}

[
Drop.e
]
The derivation has the shape

\frac{\mathsf{l}\leq\mathsf{n}\and\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}\and\rho,\mathit{q}\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{m}\odot\Gamma,\mathit{y}:\mathit{T^{\prime}}\vdash_{\mathsf{l}}\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{T}\and\rho^{\prime}\mid\mathsf{M}^{\prime}\odot\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\mathit{t^{\prime}}\colon\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{T^{\prime}}}{\rho,\rho^{\prime}\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{M}^{\prime}\odot\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\mathsf{l}}\mathbin{\mathsf{let}}_{@\mathit{q}}\operatorname{\downarrow}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{y}=\mathit{t^{\prime}}\mathbin{\mathsf{in}}\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{T}}

Write
\rho=r_{1},\mathellipsis,r_{k}
and

\rho^{\prime}=r_{1}^{\prime},\mathellipsis,r_{\ell}^{\prime}
;
Write
\Gamma=x_{1}:A_{1},\mathellipsis,x_{k}:A_{k}

and
\Gamma^{\prime}=x_{1}^{\prime}:A_{1}^{\prime},\mathellipsis,x_{\ell}^{\prime}:A_{\ell}^{\prime}
;
Write
\mathsf{M}=\mathsf{m}_{1},\mathellipsis,\mathsf{m}_{k}

and
\mathsf{M}^{\prime}=\mathsf{m}_{1}^{\prime},\mathellipsis,\mathsf{m}_{\ell}^{\prime}
.
Given terms

\sigma_{\mathit{i}}\mid\mathsf{N}_{\mathit{i}}\odot\Delta_{\mathit{i}}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}_{\mathit{i}}}\mathit{e_{\mathit{i}}}\colon\mathit{A_{\mathit{i}}}
(
i\in\{1,\mathellipsis,k\}
)
and

\sigma^{\prime}_{\mathit{i}}\mid\mathsf{N}_{\mathit{i}}\odot\Delta^{\prime}_{\mathit{i}}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}_{\mathit{i}}}\mathit{e^{\prime}_{\mathit{i}}}\colon\mathit{A^{\prime}_{\mathit{i}}}

(
i\in\{1,\mathellipsis,\ell\}
),
we must show that

\displaystyle\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\sigma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathellipsis,\mathit{r_{\mathit{k}}}\sigma_{\mathit{k}},\mathit{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\sigma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathellipsis,\mathit{r_{\mathit{k}}}\sigma^{\prime}_{\mathit{\ell}}\mid\mathsf{N}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathellipsis,\mathsf{N}_{\mathit{k}},\mathsf{N}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathellipsis,\mathsf{N}^{\prime}_{\mathit{\ell}}

\displaystyle\and\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathellipsis,\Delta_{\mathit{k}},\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathellipsis,\Delta^{\prime}_{\mathit{\ell}}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathbin{\mathsf{let}}_{@\mathit{q}}\operatorname{\downarrow}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{y}=[\mathit{e^{\prime}_{\mathit{i}}}/\mathit{x^{\prime}_{\mathit{i}}}]\mathit{t^{\prime}}\mathbin{\mathsf{in}}[\mathit{e_{\mathit{i}}}/\mathit{x_{\mathit{i}}}]\mathit{t}:T

Applying the inductive hypothesis to
t^{\prime}
, we obtain

\mathit{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\sigma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathellipsis,\mathit{r_{\mathit{k}}}\sigma^{\prime}_{\mathit{\ell}}\mid\mathsf{N}^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathellipsis,\mathsf{N}^{\prime}_{\mathit{\ell}}\odot\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathellipsis,\Delta^{\prime}_{\mathit{\ell}}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}[\mathit{e^{\prime}_{\mathit{i}}}/\mathit{x^{\prime}_{\mathit{i}}}]\mathit{t^{\prime}}\colon\mathit{T^{\prime}}.

Furthermore, applying the inductive hypothesis to
t

with
e_{1},\mathellipsis,e_{k}
and

e_{k+1}=1\mid\mathsf{m}\odot\mathit{y}:\mathit{T^{\prime}}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{y}\colon\mathit{T^{\prime}}

yields

\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\sigma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathellipsis,\mathit{r_{\mathit{k}}}\sigma_{\mathit{k}},\mathit{q}\mid\mathsf{N}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathellipsis,\mathsf{N}_{\mathit{k}},\mathsf{m}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathellipsis,\Delta_{\mathit{k}},\mathit{y}:\mathit{T^{\prime}}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}[\mathit{e_{\mathit{i}}}/\mathit{x_{\mathit{i}}}]\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{T}.

Applying the rule
Drop.e
to the two inductive hypotheses
yields the desired result.

B.2
Syntactic soundness of beta-conversions

Definition B.4
.

\beta
-conversions are defined by

\small\frac{\sigma\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\star_{\mathsf{m}}\colon\mathbf{I}_{\mathsf{m}}\hskip 9.24994pt\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{A}}{\rho,\mathit{q}\sigma\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N}\odot\Gamma,\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathbin{\mathsf{let}}_{@\mathit{q}}\star_{\mathsf{m}}=\star_{\mathsf{m}}\mathbin{\mathsf{in}}\mathit{e}\equiv_{\beta}\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{A}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={{Unit.$\beta$}}{},}\and\frac{\sigma\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}(\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\otimes\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\rho,\mathit{q},\mathit{q}\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m}\odot\Gamma,\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{A}}{\rho,\mathit{q}\sigma\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N}\odot\Gamma,\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathbin{\mathsf{let}}_{@\mathit{q}}(\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}})=(\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\mathbin{\mathsf{in}}\mathit{e}\equiv_{\beta}[\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{A}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={{Pair.$\beta$}}{},}\and\frac{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{B}^{\mathit{q}:\mathsf{m}}\multimap\mathit{A}\hskip 9.24994pt\sigma\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{B}}{\rho,\mathit{q}\sigma\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N}\odot\Gamma,\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}(\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathit{e})\,\mathit{t}\equiv_{\beta}[\mathit{t}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{A}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={{Fun.$\beta$}}{},}\and\frac{\mathit{q}\geq 1\hskip 9.24994pt\sigma\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}\,\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\oplus\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\rho,\mathit{q}\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{n}\odot\Gamma,\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\colon\mathit{A}\hskip 9.24994pt\rho,\mathit{q}\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{n}\odot\Gamma,\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\colon\mathit{A}}{\rho,\mathit{q}\sigma\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N}\odot\Gamma,\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathsf{case}_{\mathit{q}}(\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}\,\mathit{t};\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}};\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\equiv_{\beta}[\mathit{t}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\colon\mathit{A}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={{Sum.$\beta$.L}}{},}\and\frac{\mathit{q}\geq 1\hskip 9.24994pt\sigma\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\,\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\oplus\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\rho,\mathit{q}\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{n}\odot\Gamma,\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\colon\mathit{A}\hskip 9.24994pt\rho,\mathit{q}\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{n}\odot\Gamma,\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\colon\mathit{A}}{\rho,\mathit{q}\sigma\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N}\odot\Gamma,\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathsf{case}_{\mathit{q}}(\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}\,\mathit{t};\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}};\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\equiv_{\beta}[\mathit{t}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\colon\mathit{A}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={{Sum.$\beta$.R}}{},}\and\frac{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}\hskip 9.24994pt\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}}\mathit{t}\colon\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}}\mathit{A}}{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\operatorname{\uparrow}^{-1}_{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}}(\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}}\mathit{t})\equiv_{\beta}\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{A}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={{Raise.$\beta$}}{},}\and\frac{\mathsf{l}\leq\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}\hskip 9.24994pt\sigma\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{A}\hskip 9.24994pt\rho,\mathit{q}\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{m}\odot\Gamma,\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}\vdash_{\mathsf{l}}\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{B}}{\rho,\sigma\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N}\odot\Gamma,\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{l}}\mathbin{\mathsf{let}}_{@\mathit{q}}\operatorname{\downarrow}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{x}=\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\mathbin{\mathsf{in}}\mathit{e}\equiv_{\beta}[\mathit{t}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{B}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={{Drop.$\beta$}}{},}

Theorem B.5
.

\beta
-conversion preserves types and grades, i.e.
whenever

\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\equiv_{\beta}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\colon\mathit{A}

then

\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\colon\mathit{A}

and

\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\colon\mathit{A}

Proof B.6
.

In each rule,
t_{1}
is obtained from the assumptions of that rule
by applying the corresponding elimination rule.
It remains to show that
\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\colon\mathit{A}
.
We proceed by case analysis.

{case}

[
Unit.
\beta
]
We proceed by induction on the derivation of

\sigma\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\star_{\mathsf{m}}\colon\mathbf{I}_{\mathsf{m}}
.
We must consider the cases
Unit.i
and

Weak
,
Sub
,
Cont
.
All other subcases do not apply, since their derivations do not produce a
term of the form
\star_{\mathsf{m}}
.

Subcase
(
Unit.i
).
In this case the assumptions are

\emptyset\mid\emptyset\odot\emptyset\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\star_{\mathsf{m}}\colon\mathbf{I}_{\mathsf{m}}
and

\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{A}

and we must prove
\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{A}
which is an assumption.

Subcase
(
Weak
).
The derivation has the form

\frac{\mathsf{Weak}(\mathsf{n})\and\mathsf{n}\geq\mathsf{m}\and\mathit{B}\in\mathsf{Type}(\mathsf{n})\and\sigma\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\star_{\mathsf{m}}\colon\mathbf{I}_{\mathsf{m}}}{\sigma,0\mid\mathsf{N},\mathsf{n}\odot\Delta,\mathit{x}:\mathit{B}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\star_{\mathsf{m}}\colon\mathbf{I}_{\mathsf{m}}}.

The inductive hypothesis is

\rho,\mathit{q}\sigma\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N}\odot\Gamma,\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{A}

The assumptions
\mathsf{Weak}(\mathsf{n})
,
\mathsf{n}\geq\mathsf{m}
and
\mathit{B}\in\mathsf{Type}(\mathsf{n})

guarantee that we can apply weakening to the IH to obtain the desired result,
noticing that
\mathit{q}(\sigma,0)=(\mathit{q}\sigma),0
.

Subcase
(
Sub
).
The derivation is

\frac{\sigma\leq\sigma^{\prime}\and\sigma\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\star_{\mathsf{m}}\colon\mathbf{I}_{\mathsf{m}}}{\sigma^{\prime}\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\star_{\mathsf{m}}\colon\mathbf{I}_{\mathsf{m}}}

with IH
\rho,\mathit{q}\sigma\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N}\odot\Gamma,\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\star_{\mathsf{m}}\colon\mathbf{I}_{\mathsf{m}}
.
From
\sigma\leq\sigma^{\prime}
we obtain
\rho,\mathit{q}\sigma\leq\rho,\mathit{q}\sigma^{\prime}
,
so applying the rule
Sub
to the IH yields the
desired result.

Subcase
(
Cont
).
In this case the derivation has the form

\frac{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\in\mathsf{Cont}(\mathsf{n})\and\sigma,\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mid\mathsf{N},\mathsf{n},\mathsf{n}\odot\Delta,\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{B},\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{B}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\star_{\mathsf{m}}\colon\mathbf{I}_{\mathsf{m}}}{\sigma,\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mid\mathsf{N},\mathsf{n}\odot\Delta,\mathit{z}:\mathit{B}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\star_{\mathsf{m}}\colon\mathbf{I}_{\mathsf{m}}}

The IH is

\rho,\mathit{q}\sigma,\mathit{q}\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{q}\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N},\mathsf{n},\mathsf{n}\odot\Gamma,\Delta,\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{B},\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{B}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{A}

The variables
y_{1},y_{2}
do not occur freely in
e
,
because we have the derivation
\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{A}
.
Therefore, it suffices to show that

\rho,\mathit{q}\sigma,\mathit{q}(\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N},\mathsf{n}\odot\Gamma,\Delta,\mathit{z}:\mathit{B}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{A}

Since
\mathsf{Cont}(\mathsf{n})
is an ideal, we have
\mathit{q}\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{q}\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\in\mathsf{Cont}(\mathsf{n})
,
and hence may apply the rule
Cont

to obtain the desired result.

{case}

[
Pair.
\beta
]
Given

\rho,\mathit{q},\mathit{q}\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m}\odot\Gamma,\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{A}\and\sigma\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}(\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\otimes\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}},

we must show that

\rho,\mathit{q}\sigma\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N}\odot\Gamma,\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}[\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{A}.

Proceed by induction on the derivation of

\sigma\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}(\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\otimes\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.
We must consider the cases
Pair.i
and

Weak
,
Sub
,
Cont
.
All other subcases do not apply since they don’t produce a term of the form

(\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}})
.

Subcase
(
Pair.i
).
In this case the derivation is

\frac{\sigma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mid\mathsf{N}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\and\sigma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mid\mathsf{N}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}{\sigma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\sigma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mid\mathsf{N}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathsf{N}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}(\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\otimes\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}

Applying the substitution theorem with
t_{1}
and
t_{2}
,
we obtain

\rho,\mathit{q}\sigma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{q}\sigma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathsf{N}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot\Gamma,\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}[\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{A}

as desired.

Subcase
(
Weak
).
In this case the derivation is

\frac{\mathsf{n}^{\prime}\geq\mathsf{m}\and\mathsf{Weak}(\mathsf{n}^{\prime})\and\mathit{B}\in\mathsf{Type}(\mathsf{n}^{\prime})\and\sigma\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}(\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\otimes\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}{\sigma,0\mid\mathsf{N},\mathsf{n}^{\prime}\odot\Delta,\mathit{z}:\mathit{B}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}(\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\otimes\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}

By induction we have

\rho,\mathit{q}\sigma\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N}\odot\Gamma,\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}[\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{A}.

We have
\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}^{\prime}
and hence may apply the rule

Weak
to the IH to get

\rho,\mathit{q}\sigma,0\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N},\mathsf{n}^{\prime}\odot\Gamma,\Delta,\mathit{z}:\mathit{B}\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}[\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{A}

as desired.

Subcase
(
Sub
).
The derivation is

\frac{\sigma^{\prime}\leq\sigma\and\sigma^{\prime}\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}(\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\otimes\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}{\sigma\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}(\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\otimes\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}

and the inductive hypothesis is

\rho,\mathit{q}\sigma^{\prime}\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N}\odot\Gamma,\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}[\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{A}.

The desired result follows by applying the rule
Sub

using the fact that multiplcation is monotone.

Subcase
(
Cont
).
The derivation is

\frac{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\in\mathsf{Cont}(\mathsf{n}^{\prime})\and\sigma,\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mid\mathsf{N},\mathsf{n}^{\prime},\mathsf{n}^{\prime}\odot\Delta,\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{B},\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{B}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}(\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\otimes\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}{\sigma,\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mid\mathsf{N},\mathsf{n}^{\prime},\mathsf{n}^{\prime}\odot\Delta,\mathit{z}:\mathit{B}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}([\mathit{z}/\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{z}/\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}},[\mathit{z}/\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{z}/\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\otimes\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}

The IH is

\rho,\mathit{q}\sigma,\mathit{q}\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{q}\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N},\mathsf{n}^{\prime},\mathsf{n}^{\prime}\odot\Gamma,\Delta,\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{B},\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{B}\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}[\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{A}

Since
\mathsf{Cont}(\mathsf{n}^{\prime})
is an ideal, we have
\mathit{q}\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{q}\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\in\mathsf{Cont}(\mathsf{n}^{\prime})
.
We thus apply the rule
Cont

to obtain

\rho,\mathit{q}\sigma,\mathit{q}\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{q}\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N},\mathsf{n}^{\prime}\odot\Gamma,\Delta,\mathit{z}:\mathit{B}\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}[\mathit{z}/\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{z}/\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}][\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{A}

Since the variables
y_{1},y_{2}
only occur freely in
t_{1}
and
t_{2}
,
the resulting term is equal to

[[\mathit{z}/\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{z}/\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},[\mathit{z}/\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{z}/\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{e}

as desired.

{case}

[
Fun.
\beta
]
Given

\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{B}^{\mathit{q}:\mathsf{m}}\multimap\mathit{A}\and\sigma\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{B}

we must prove that

\rho,\mathit{q}\sigma\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N}\odot\Gamma,\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}[\mathit{t}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{A}

We proceed by induction on the derivation of

\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{B}^{\mathit{q}:\mathsf{m}}\multimap\mathit{A}.

We must analyse the cases
Fun.i
and

Weak
,
Sub
,
Cont
.
The other cases do not apply, since they do not produce a term of the form

\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathit{e}
.

Subcase
(
Fun.i
).
In this case we have

\frac{\rho,\mathit{q}\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{n}\odot\Gamma,\mathit{x}:\mathit{B}\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{A}}{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{B}^{\mathit{q}:\mathsf{m}}\multimap\mathit{A}}

The claim follows by applying substitution to the assumption

\rho,\mathit{q}\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{n}\odot\Gamma,\mathit{x}:\mathit{B}\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{A}

Subcase
(
Weak
).
In this case the derivation is

\frac{\mathsf{Weak}(\mathsf{m}^{\prime})\and\mathsf{m}^{\prime}\geq\mathsf{n}\and\mathit{T}\in\mathsf{Type}(\mathsf{m}^{\prime})\and\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{B}^{\mathit{q}:\mathsf{m}}\multimap\mathit{A}}{\rho,0\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}\odot\Gamma,\mathit{y}:\mathit{T}\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{B}^{\mathit{q}:\mathsf{m}}\multimap\mathit{A}}

By induction we have

\rho,\mathit{q}\sigma\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N}\odot\Gamma,\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}[\mathit{t}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{A}

and we may apply the rule
Weak

to obtain

\rho,\mathit{q}\sigma,0\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}\odot\Gamma,\Delta,\mathit{y}:\mathit{T}\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}[\mathit{t}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{A}

Since we treat contexts as unoredered, this is equivalent to the desired
judgment

\rho,0,\mathit{q}\sigma\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{m}^{\prime},\mathsf{N}\odot\Gamma,\mathit{y}:\mathit{T},\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}[\mathit{t}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{A}

Subcase
(
Cont
).
In this case the derivation is

\frac{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\in\mathsf{Cont}(\mathsf{m}^{\prime})\and\rho,\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{m}^{\prime},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}\odot\Gamma,\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{T},\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{T}\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{B}^{\mathit{q}:\mathsf{m}}\multimap\mathit{A}}{\rho,\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{m}^{\prime},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}\odot\Gamma,\mathit{z}:\mathit{T}\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}(\lambda\mathit{x}.[\mathit{z}/\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{z}/\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{e})\colon\mathit{B}^{\mathit{q}:\mathsf{m}}\multimap\mathit{A}}

By induction we have

\rho,\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\mathit{q}\sigma\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{m}^{\prime},\mathsf{m}^{\prime},\mathsf{N}\odot\Gamma,\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{T},\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{T},\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}[\mathit{t}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{A}

Since contexts are unordered, we may apply the rule

Cont
to obtain

\rho,\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\mathit{q}\sigma\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{m}^{\prime},\mathsf{N}\odot\Gamma,\mathit{z}:\mathit{T},\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}[\mathit{z}/\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{z}/\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}][\mathit{t}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{A}

Since the variables
y_{1},y_{2}
are not free in
t
this term is equal to

[\mathit{t}/\mathit{x}][\mathit{z}/\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{z}/\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{e}
which is the desired term.

Subcase
(
Sub
).
In this case we have the derivation

\frac{\rho^{\prime}\leq\rho\and\rho^{\prime}\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{B}^{\mathit{q}:\mathsf{m}}\multimap\mathit{A}}{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{B}^{\mathit{q}:\mathsf{m}}\multimap\mathit{A}}

The inductive hypothesis is

\rho^{\prime},\mathit{q}\sigma\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N}\odot\Gamma,\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}[\mathit{t}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{A}

and since
\rho^{\prime},\mathit{q}\sigma\leq\rho,\mathit{q}\sigma
we may apply
the rule
Sub
to obtain the desired result.

{case}

[
Raise.
\beta
]
For modes
\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}
,
given
\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}}\mathit{t}\colon\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}}\mathit{A}
,
we must prove that
\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{A}
.
We proceed by induction on the derivation of

\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}}\mathit{t}\colon\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}}\mathit{A}
.
We must analyse the cases
Raise.i
and

Weak
,
Sub
,
Cont
.

Subcase
(
Raise.i
).
In this case the derivation is

\frac{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}\and\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{A}}{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}}\mathit{t}\colon\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}}\mathit{A}}.

We are done, since our goal is an assumption.

Subcase
(
Weak
).
In this case the derivation is

\frac{\mathsf{Weak}(\mathsf{m}^{\prime})\and\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}^{\prime}\and\mathit{B}\in\mathsf{Type}(\mathsf{m}^{\prime})\and\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}}\mathit{t}\colon\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}}\mathit{A}}{\rho,0\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}\odot\Gamma,\mathit{x}:\mathit{B}\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}}\mathit{t}\colon\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}}\mathit{A}}

The IH is
\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{A}
.
Because of
\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
we may apply
Weak

to the IH to obtain

\rho,0\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}\odot\Gamma,\mathit{x}:\mathit{B}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{A}
as desired.

Subcase
(
Cont
).
In this case the derivation is

\frac{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\in\mathsf{Cont}(\mathsf{m}^{\prime})\and\rho,\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{m}^{\prime},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}\odot\Gamma,\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{B},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{B}\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}}\mathit{t}\colon\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}}\mathit{A}}{\rho,\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}\odot\Gamma,\mathit{z}:\mathit{B}\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}}[\mathit{z}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{z}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{t}\colon\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}}\mathit{A}}.

The IH is

\rho,\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{m}^{\prime},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}\odot\Gamma,\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{B},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{B}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{A}

and we still have
\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\in\mathsf{Cont}(\mathsf{m}^{\prime})

and thus may apply the rule
Cont
to obtain

\rho,\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}\odot\Gamma,\mathit{z}:\mathit{B}\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}[\mathit{z}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{z}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{A}

which is the desired term.

Subcase
(
Sub
).
The derivation has the form

\frac{\rho^{\prime}\leq\rho\and\rho^{\prime}\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}}\mathit{t}\colon\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}}\mathit{A}}{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}}\mathit{t}\colon\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{m}\leq\mathsf{n}}\mathit{A}}

The IH is

\rho^{\prime}\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{A}
and applying rule
Sub

with
\rho^{\prime}\leq\rho
produces the desired term.

{case}

[
Drop.
\beta
]
Given
\mathsf{l}\leq\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}

and

\sigma\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{A}\and\rho,\mathit{q}\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{m}\odot\Gamma,\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}\vdash_{\mathsf{l}}\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{B},

we must show that

\rho,\sigma\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N}\odot\Gamma,\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{l}}[\mathit{t}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{B}.

We proceed by induction on the derivation of

\sigma\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{A}
.
We must analyse the cases
Drop.i
and

Weak
,
Sub
,
Cont
.

Subcase
(
Drop.i
).
In this case there exists a grade vector
\sigma^{\prime}
with

\mathit{q}\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma
such that the dervation is

\frac{\sigma^{\prime}\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{A}}{\mathit{q}\sigma^{\prime}\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{A}}

Apply substitution with the judgment
\sigma^{\prime}\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{A}

to obtain the desired term.

Subcase
(
Weak
).
In this case the derivation is

\frac{\mathsf{Weak}(\mathsf{n}^{\prime})\and\mathsf{n}^{\prime}\geq\mathsf{n}\and\mathit{T}\in\mathsf{Type}(\mathsf{n}^{\prime})\and\sigma\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{A}}{\sigma,0\mid\mathsf{N},\mathsf{n}^{\prime}\odot\Delta,\mathit{y}:\mathit{T}\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{A}}

The inductive hypothesis is

\rho,\sigma\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N}\odot\Gamma,\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{l}}[\mathit{t}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{B}.

and since we have
\mathsf{n}^{\prime}\geq\mathsf{n}\geq\mathsf{l}
we may apply weakening to obtain

\rho,\sigma,0\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N},\mathsf{n}^{\prime}\odot\Gamma,\Delta,\mathit{y}:\mathit{T}\vdash_{\mathsf{l}}[\mathit{t}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{B}.

Subcase
(
Sub
).
In this case the derivation is

\frac{\sigma^{\prime}\leq\sigma\and\sigma^{\prime}\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{A}}{\sigma\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{A}}

From
\sigma^{\prime}\leq\sigma
we obtain
\rho,\sigma^{\prime}\leq\rho,\sigma

and hence we may apply the rule
Sub

to the inductive hypothesis

\rho,\sigma^{\prime}\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N}\odot\Gamma,\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{l}}[\mathit{t}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{B}.

to obtain the desired term.

Subcase
(
Cont
).
In this case the derivation is

\frac{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\in\mathsf{Cont}(\mathsf{n}^{\prime})\and\sigma,\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mid\mathsf{N},\mathsf{n}^{\prime},\mathsf{n}^{\prime}\odot\Delta,\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{T},\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{T}\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{A}}{\sigma,\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mid\mathsf{N},\mathsf{n}^{\prime}\odot\Delta,\mathit{z}:\mathit{T}\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}[\mathit{z}/\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{z}/\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{t}\colon\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{A}}

The inductive hypothesis is

\rho,\sigma,\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N},\mathsf{n}^{\prime},\mathsf{n}^{\prime}\odot\Gamma,\Delta,\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{T},\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{T}\vdash_{\mathsf{l}}[\mathit{t}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{B}.

Applying
Cont
we obtain

\rho,\sigma,\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N},\mathsf{n}^{\prime}\odot\Gamma,\Delta,\mathit{z}:\mathit{T}\vdash_{\mathsf{l}}[\mathit{z}/\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{z}/\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}][\mathit{t}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{B}.

Since the variables
y_{1},y_{2}
are not free in
e
,
this is equal to the term
[[\mathit{z}/\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{z}/\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{t}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{e}
,
which is the desired term.

{case}

[
Sum.
\beta
.L
]
Given

\mathit{q}\geq 1\and\rho,\mathit{q}\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{n}\odot\Gamma,\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\colon\mathit{A}\and\sigma\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}\,\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\oplus\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}

we must show that

\rho,\mathit{q}\sigma\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N}\odot\Gamma,\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}[\mathit{t}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\colon\mathit{A}

We proceed by induction on the derivation of

\sigma\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}\,\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\oplus\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.
We must analyse the cases
Sum.il
and

Weak
,
Sub
,
Cont
.

Subcase
(
Sum.il
).
In this case the derivation is

\frac{\sigma\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}{\sigma\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}\,\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\oplus\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}

Apply substitution to obtain the desired result.

Subcase
(
Weak
).
In this cae the derivation is

\frac{\mathsf{Weak}(\mathsf{n}^{\prime})\and\mathsf{n}^{\prime}\geq\mathsf{n}\and\mathit{T}\in\mathsf{Type}(\mathsf{n}^{\prime})\and\sigma\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}\,\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\oplus\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}{\sigma,0\mid\mathsf{N},\mathsf{n}^{\prime}\odot\Delta,\mathit{y}:\mathit{T}\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}\,\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\oplus\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}

We have
\mathsf{n}^{\prime}\geq\mathsf{n}\geq\mathsf{m}
and hence may apply the rule

Weak
to the inductive hypothesis,
obtaining

\frac{\rho,\mathit{q}\sigma\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N}\odot\Gamma,\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}[\mathit{t}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\colon\mathit{A}}{\rho,\mathit{q}\sigma,0\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N},\mathsf{n}^{\prime}\odot\Gamma,\Delta,\mathit{y}:\mathit{T}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}[\mathit{t}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\colon\mathit{A}}

as desired.

Subcase
(
Sub
).
In this case the derivation is

\frac{\sigma^{\prime}\leq\sigma\and\sigma^{\prime}\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}\,\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\oplus\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}{\sigma\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}\,\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\oplus\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}

By monotonicity of multiplication we have

\rho,\mathit{q}\sigma^{\prime}\leq\rho,\mathit{q}\sigma
and hence
we may apply the rule
Sub

to the inductive hypothesis

\rho,\mathit{q}\sigma^{\prime}\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N}\odot\Gamma,\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}[\mathit{t}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\colon\mathit{A}

obtaining the desired result.

Subcase
(
Cont
).
In this case the derivation is

\frac{\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\in\mathsf{Cont}(\mathsf{n}^{\prime})\and\sigma,\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mid\mathsf{N},\mathsf{n}^{\prime},\mathsf{n}^{\prime}\odot\Delta,\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{T},\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{T}\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}\,\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\oplus\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}{\sigma,\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mid\mathsf{N},\mathsf{n}^{\prime}\odot\Delta,\mathit{z}:\mathit{T}\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}\,[\mathit{z}/\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{z}/\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\oplus\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}

The inductive hypothesis is

\rho,\mathit{q}(\sigma,\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N},\mathsf{n}^{\prime},\mathsf{n}^{\prime}\odot\Gamma,\Delta,\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{T},\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{T}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}[\mathit{t}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\colon\mathit{A}.

Since
\mathsf{Cont}(\mathsf{n}^{\prime})
is an ideal,
we have
\mathit{q}\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{q}\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\in\mathsf{Cont}(\mathsf{n}^{\prime})

and thus may apply the rule
Cont

to obtain

\rho,\mathit{q}(\sigma,\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{N},\mathsf{n}^{\prime}\odot\Gamma,\Delta,\mathit{z}:\mathit{T}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}[\mathit{z}/\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{z}/\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}][\mathit{t}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\colon\mathit{A}.

Since the variables
y_{1},y_{2}
are not free in
e_{1}
,
this term is equal to
[[\mathit{z}/\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{z}/\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{t}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
,
concluding the proof.

{case}

[
Sum.
\beta
.R
]
Analogous to the case
Sum.
\beta
.L
.

B.3
Syntactic soundness eta-conversions

We define
\eta
-conversion as a set of typed equalities as follows:

\frac{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\mathbf{I}_{\mathsf{m}}}{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\equiv_{\eta}\mathbin{\mathsf{let}}_{@1}\star_{\mathsf{m}}=\mathit{t}\mathbin{\mathsf{in}}\star_{\mathsf{m}}\colon\mathbf{I}_{\mathsf{m}}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={{Unit.$\eta$}}{},}\and\frac{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\otimes\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\equiv_{\eta}\mathbin{\mathsf{let}}_{@1}(\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}})=\mathit{t}\mathbin{\mathsf{in}}(\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\otimes\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={{Pair.$\eta$}}{},}\and\frac{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{A}^{\mathit{q}:\mathsf{m}}\multimap\mathit{B}}{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\equiv_{\eta}\lambda\mathit{x}.(\mathit{t}\,\mathit{x})\colon\mathit{A}^{\mathit{q}:\mathsf{m}}\multimap\mathit{B}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={{Fun.$\eta$}}{},}\and\frac{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\oplus\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\equiv_{\eta}\mathsf{case}_{1}(\mathit{t};\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}\,\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}};\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\,\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\oplus\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={{Sum.$\eta$}}{},}\and\frac{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{A}}{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\equiv_{\eta}\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}(\operatorname{\uparrow}^{-1}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t})\colon\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{A}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={{Raise.$\eta$}}{},}\and\frac{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\mathit{t}\colon\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{A}}{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\mathit{t}\equiv_{\eta}\mathbin{\mathsf{let}}_{@\mathit{q}}\operatorname{\downarrow}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{x}=\mathit{t}\mathbin{\mathsf{in}}\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{x}\colon\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{A}}\quad\textsc{narrower=0.3,lab={{Drop.$\eta$}}{},}

Theorem B.7
.

\eta
-conversion preserves types and grades:
Whenever
\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\equiv_{\eta}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\colon\mathit{A}

then
\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\colon\mathit{A}
and

\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\colon\mathit{A}
.

Proof B.8
.

The assertion that
\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\colon\mathit{A}

is an assumption in each rule for
\eta
-expansion.

For the assertion that
\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\colon\mathit{A}
we have the following
derivations:

\frac{\frac{}{\emptyset\mid\emptyset\odot\emptyset\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\star_{\mathsf{m}}\colon\mathbf{I}_{\mathsf{m}}}\quad\textsc{Left=\text{{Unit.i}{}}}\and\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\mathbf{I}_{\mathsf{m}}}{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathbin{\mathsf{let}}_{@1}\star_{\mathsf{m}}=\mathit{t}\mathbin{\mathsf{in}}\star_{\mathsf{m}}\colon\mathbf{I}_{\mathsf{m}}}\quad\textsc{Left = \text{{Unit.e}{}}}\\
\frac{\frac{\frac{}{1\mid\mathsf{m}\odot\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Left = \text{{Var}{}}}\and\frac{}{1\mid\mathsf{m}\odot\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Left = \text{{Var}{}}}}{1,1\mid\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m}\odot\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}(\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\otimes\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\quad\textsc{Left = \text{{Pair.i}{}}}\and\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\otimes\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathbin{\mathsf{let}}_{@1}(\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}})=\mathit{t}\mathbin{\mathsf{in}}(\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\otimes\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\quad\textsc{Left = \text{{Pair.e}{}}}\\
\frac{\frac{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{A}^{\mathit{q}:\mathsf{m}}\multimap\mathit{B}\and\frac{}{1\mid\mathsf{m}\odot\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{x}\colon\mathit{A}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Left = \text{{Var}{}}}}{\rho,\mathit{q}\mid\mathsf{M},\mathsf{m}\odot\Gamma,\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\mathit{t}\,\mathit{x}\colon\mathit{B}}\quad\textsc{Left = \text{{Fun.e}{}}}}{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\lambda\mathit{x}.(\mathit{t}\,\mathit{x})\colon\mathit{A}^{\mathit{q}:\mathsf{m}}\multimap\mathit{B}}\quad\textsc{Left = \text{{Fun.i}{}}}\\
\frac{\frac{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{A}}{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\uparrow}^{-1}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{A}}\quad\textsc{Left = \text{{Raise.e}{}}}}{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}(\operatorname{\uparrow}^{-1}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t})\colon\operatorname{\uparrow}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{A}}\quad\textsc{Left = \text{{Raise.i}{}}}\\
\frac{\frac{\frac{}{1\mid\mathsf{m}\odot\mathit{z}:\mathit{A}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{z}\colon\mathit{A}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Left = \text{{Var}{}}}}{\mathit{q}\mid\mathsf{m}\odot\mathit{z}:\mathit{A}\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{z}\colon\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{A}}\quad\textsc{Left = \text{{Drop.i}{}}}\and\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\mathit{t}\colon\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{A}}{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\mathbin{\mathsf{let}}_{@\mathit{q}}\operatorname{\downarrow}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{z}=\mathit{t}\mathbin{\mathsf{in}}\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{z}\colon\operatorname{\downarrow}^{\mathit{q}}_{\mathsf{n}\leq\mathsf{m}}\mathit{A}}\quad\textsc{Left = \text{{Drop.e}{}}}\\
\frac{\frac{\frac{}{1\mid\mathsf{m}\odot\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Left=\text{{Var}{}}}}{1\mid\mathsf{m}\odot\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}\,\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\oplus\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\quad\textsc{Left=\text{{Sum.il}{}}}\ \frac{\frac{}{1\mid\mathsf{m}\odot\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Left=\text{{Var}{}}}}{1\mid\mathsf{m}\odot\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\,\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\oplus\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\quad\textsc{left=\text{{Sum.ir}{}}}\and\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\oplus\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}{\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{m}}\mathit{t}\equiv_{\eta}\mathsf{case}_{1}(\mathit{t};\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}\,\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}};\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\,\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\colon\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\oplus\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\quad\textsc{Left = \text{{Sum.e}{}}}

Appendix C
Semantic soundness of the equational theory

C.1
Substitution is composition

Lemma C.1
.

In the situation of Theorem
B.2
,
suppose the terms
t
and
e_{1},\mathellipsis,e_{k}
have categorical
semantics

\llbracket\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\and\Gamma\rrbracket_{\mathsf{m}}=\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}(r_{i}\and A_{i})\xrightarrow{t}B\and\llbracket\sigma_{i}\mid\mathsf{N}_{i}\and\Delta_{i}\rrbracket_{\mathsf{m}_{i}}=\bigotimes_{j}F^{\mathsf{n}_{ij}}_{\mathsf{m}_{i}}(q_{ij}\and A_{ij})\xrightarrow{e_{i}}A_{i}\ (i\in\{1,\mathellipsis,k\})

The interpretation of the substition

\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\sigma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathellipsis,\mathit{r_{\mathit{k}}}\sigma_{\mathit{k}}\mid\mathsf{N}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathellipsis,\mathsf{N}_{\mathit{k}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathellipsis,\Delta_{\mathit{k}}\vdash_{\mathsf{l}}[\mathit{e_{\mathit{i}}}/\mathit{x_{\mathit{i}}}]\mathit{t}\colon\mathit{B}

is given by the morphism

\bigotimes_{i}\llbracket\mathit{r_{\mathit{i}}}\sigma_{\mathit{i}}\mid\mathsf{N}_{\mathit{i}}\odot\Delta_{\mathit{i}}\rrbracket_{\mathsf{m}}\cong\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}\llbracket\mathit{r_{\mathit{i}}}\sigma_{\mathit{i}}\mid\mathsf{N}_{\mathit{i}}\odot\Delta_{\mathit{i}}\rrbracket_{\mathsf{m}_{\mathit{i}}}\xrightarrow{\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}(r_{i}\cdot e_{i})}\llbracket\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\rrbracket_{\mathsf{m}}\xrightarrow{t}B

or, equivalently:

\bigotimes_{i}\bigotimes_{j}F^{\mathsf{n}_{ij}}_{\mathsf{m}}(r_{i}q_{ij}\and A_{ij})\cong\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}\bigotimes_{j}F^{\mathsf{n}_{ij}}_{\mathsf{m}_{i}}(r_{i}q_{ij}\and A_{ij})\xrightarrow{\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}(r_{i}\cdot e_{i})}\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}(r_{i}\and A_{i})\xrightarrow{t}B

Before the proof, we begin with a lemma.

Lemma C.2
.

In the situation of the above lemma,
suppose that
s\in R_{\mathsf{m}}
and
write
f
for the composite

\bigotimes_{ij}F^{\mathsf{n}_{ij}}_{\mathsf{m}}(r_{i}q_{ij}\and A_{ij})\cong\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}\bigotimes_{j}F^{\mathsf{n}_{ij}}_{\mathsf{m}_{i}}(r_{i}q_{ij}\and A_{ij})\xrightarrow{\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}(r_{i}\cdot e_{i})}\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}(r_{i}\and A_{i})\xrightarrow{t}B.

The morphism
s\cdot f
is equal to the composite

\bigotimes_{ij}F^{\mathsf{n}_{ij}}_{\mathsf{m}}(sr_{i}q_{ij}\and A_{ij})\cong\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}\bigotimes_{j}F^{\mathsf{n}_{ij}}_{\mathsf{m}_{i}}(sr_{i}q_{ij}\and A_{ij})\xrightarrow{\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}(sr_{i}\cdot e_{i})}\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}(sr_{i}\and A_{i})\xrightarrow{(s\cdot t)}(s\and B)

Proof C.3
.

Consider the following diagram
in which unlabelled arrows are assembled from
\mu
,
\delta
and
\tau
,
to “move scalars out of the tensor product,”
and the vertical arrows labeled “
\sim
” are induced by the isomorphisms

F^{\mathsf{n}_{ij}}_{\mathsf{m}}\cong F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}\circ F^{\mathsf{n}_{ij}}_{\mathsf{m}_{i}}

and the strong monoidal structures on the functors
F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimes_{ij}F^{\mathsf{n}_{ij}}_{\mathsf{m}}(sr_{i}q_{ij}\and A_{ij})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\and\bigotimes_{ij}F^{\mathsf{n}_{ij}}_{\mathsf{m}}(r_{i}q_{ij}\and A_{ij})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}\bigotimes_{j}F^{\mathsf{n}_{ij}}_{\mathsf{m}_{i}}(sr_{i}q_{ij}\and A_{ij})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\and\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}\bigotimes_{j}F^{\mathsf{n}_{ij}}_{\mathsf{m}_{i}}(r_{i}q_{ij}\and A_{ij})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}\big(sr_{i}\and\bigotimes_{j}F^{\mathsf{n}_{ij}}_{\mathsf{m}_{i}}(q_{ij}\and A_{ij})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\and\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}\big(r_{i}\and\bigotimes_{j}F^{\mathsf{n}_{ij}}_{\mathsf{m}_{i}}(q_{ij}\and A_{ij})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}(sr_{i}\and A_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\and\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}(r_{i}\and A_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\and B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}(sr_{i}\and e_{i})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\and\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}(r_{i}\and e_{i})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\and t}

The composite of the top and right edge is
s\cdot f
,
while the composite of the left and bottom edge is the morphism from the claim.
The bottom square commutes by naturality of
\mu
,
\delta
and
\tau
.
The middle square commutes by the coherence conditions on
\mu
;
in fact those conditions imply that all morphisms assembled from
\delta
,

\mu
and
\tau
beginning at the top left of that square
at the bottom right of that square are equal.
The top square commutes by the naturality of the involved transformations,
using that
\mu
is a monoidal natural transformation.
Thus the diagram commutes, concluding the proof.

Proof C.4

(Of Lemma
C.1
)
.

The proof of Theorem
B.2

gives a constructive transformation of the derivations of
t
and the
e_{i}

into a derivation of
[\mathit{e_{\mathit{i}}}/\mathit{x_{\mathit{i}}}]\mathit{t}
.
We proceed by induction on the derivation of
t
,
inspecting the proof of Theorem
B.2
.

{case}

[
Var
]
In this case we must verify that any morphism

\llbracket\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\rrbracket_{\mathsf{m}}\xrightarrow{t}B

is equal to

F^{\mathsf{m}}_{\mathsf{m}}\llbracket 1\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\odot\Gamma\rrbracket_{\mathsf{m}}\xrightarrow{F^{\mathsf{m}}_{\mathsf{m}}(1\cdot t)}F^{\mathsf{m}}_{\mathsf{m}}(1\and B)\cong 1\and B\xrightarrow{\epsilon}B

which follows from the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}(1\cdot r_{i}\and A_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}(1\and r_{i}\and A_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimes_{i}1\and F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}(r_{i}\and A_{i})}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}(r_{i}\and A_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}(r_{i}\and A_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}(r_{i}\and A_{i})}

{case}

[
Weak
]
Consider a term

\sigma\mid\mathsf{N}\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{n}}\mathit{e}\colon\mathit{T}

with categorical interpretation

\llbracket\sigma\mid\mathsf{N}\and\Delta\rrbracket_{\mathsf{n}}=\bigotimes_{j}F^{\mathsf{n}_{j}}_{\mathsf{n}}(s_{j}\and Y_{j})\xrightarrow{e}T

Consider the following morphism:

\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}\llbracket\sigma_{i}\mid\mathsf{N}_{i}\and\Delta_{i}\rrbracket_{\mathsf{m}_{i}}\otimes F^{\mathsf{n}}_{\mathsf{m}}\Big(\bigotimes_{j}F^{\mathsf{n}_{j}}_{\mathsf{n}}(0s_{j}\and Y_{j})\Big)\\
\xrightarrow{\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}(r_{i}\cdot e_{i})\otimes F^{\mathsf{n}}_{\mathsf{m}}(0\cdot e)}\llbracket\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\and\Gamma\rrbracket_{\mathsf{m}}\otimes F^{\mathsf{n}}_{\mathsf{m}}(0\and T)\xrightarrow{t\otimes F^{\mathsf{n}}_{\mathsf{m}}w_{T}}B\otimes F^{\mathsf{n}}_{\mathsf{m}}(I)\cong B

This morphism is the morphism we claim realizes weakening by a variable of type

T
and then substituting the term
e
for that variable.
On the other hand, the proof of Theorem
B.2
,
constructs this term as a sequence of weakenings,
which has the following interpretation:

\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}\llbracket\sigma_{i}\mid\mathsf{N}_{i}\and\Delta_{i}\rrbracket_{\mathsf{m}_{i}}\otimes\bigotimes_{j}F^{\mathsf{n}_{j}}_{\mathsf{m}}(0\cdot s_{j}\and Y_{j})\\
\xrightarrow{\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}(r_{i}\cdot e_{i})\otimes\bigotimes_{j}F^{\mathsf{n}_{j}}_{\mathsf{m}}(w_{Y_{j}})}\llbracket\rho\mid\mathsf{M}\and\Gamma\rrbracket_{\mathsf{m}}\otimes\bigotimes_{j}F^{\mathsf{n}_{j}}_{\mathsf{m}}I\xrightarrow{t\otimes\bigotimes_{j}u^{-1}}B\otimes\bigotimes_{j}I\cong B

Our task is therefore to prove that the two composites above are equal.
This boils down to showing the commutation of the diagram below,
in which the composition across the left edge is the definition of
0\cdot e
.
The top square commutes by the coherence conditions on exponential actions,
the second square due to the coherence conditions on
\mu
,
the third square due to the fact that
w
is a monoidal natural transformation
and the last square by naturality of
w
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimes_{j}F^{\mathsf{n}_{j}}_{\mathsf{n}}(0s_{j}\and Y_{j})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimes_{j}I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimes_{j}F^{\mathsf{n}_{j}}_{\mathsf{n}}(0\and s_{j}\and Y_{j})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimes_{j}I}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimes_{j}I}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimes_{j}\mu}

{case}

[
Sub
]
Suppose we have

\rho^{\prime}=r_{1}^{\prime},\mathellipsis,r_{k}^{\prime}\geq r_{1},\mathellipsis,r_{k}=\rho
.
Consider the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimes_{i,j}F^{\mathsf{n}_{ij}}_{\mathsf{m}}(r_{i}q_{ij}\and A_{ij})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimes_{i,j}F^{\mathsf{n}_{ij}}_{\mathsf{m}}(r_{i}^{\prime}q_{ij}\and A_{ij})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}\bigotimes_{j}F^{\mathsf{n}_{ij}}_{\mathsf{m}_{i}}(r_{i}q_{ij}\and A_{ij})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}\bigotimes_{j}F^{\mathsf{n}_{ij}}_{\mathsf{m}_{i}}(r_{i}^{\prime}q_{ij}\and A_{ij})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}\big(r_{i}\and\bigotimes_{j}F^{\mathsf{n}_{ij}}_{\mathsf{m}_{i}}(q_{ij}\and A_{ij})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}\big(r_{i}^{\prime}\and\bigotimes_{j}F^{\mathsf{n}_{ij}}_{\mathsf{m}_{i}}(q_{ij}\and A_{ij})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}(r_{i}\and A_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}(r_{i}^{\prime}\and A_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}(\tau\circ\bigotimes_{j}(\mu\circ F^{\mathsf{n}_{ij}}_{\mathsf{m}_{i}}(\delta)))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}(\tau\circ\bigotimes_{j}(\mu\circ F^{\mathsf{n}_{ij}}_{\mathsf{m}_{i}}(\delta)))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}(r_{i}\and e_{i})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigotimes_{i}F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}(r_{i}^{\prime}\and e_{i})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}

Where all horizontal arrows are induced by
\rho^{\prime}\geq\rho
.
By the inductive hypothesis,
the composite across the left edge is the interpretation
of the term
[\mathit{e_{\mathit{i}}}/\mathit{x_{\mathit{i}}}]\mathit{t}
.
Since the interpretation of the
Sub
rule is given
by precomposing with the action of
\rho^{\prime}\geq\rho
,
the composite of the left edge with the topmost horizontal morphism is the
interpetation of applying
Sub
to the the interpretation
of
[\mathit{e_{\mathit{i}}}/\mathit{x_{\mathit{i}}}]\mathit{t}
.
The composite across the right edge of the diagram is the morphism from our claim,
so we must show that this diagram commutes.
The top two squares commute by the naturality of the involved vertical morphisms,
the third square commutes by the functoriality of the
F^{\mathsf{m}_{i}}_{\mathsf{m}}
.
The bottom triangle commutes by definition of the interpretation of the

rule.
